Audit of Your Semi-Automatic Annotation Workflow | Using spectral differencing to seed candidate polygons speeds up labeling, but naive thresholding introduces **selection bias** (missing subtle changes that fail the threshold) and **label leakage**. |  | - **Enforce Blind Verification (No Future OSM Peeking).** Only cross-reference OpenStreetMap (OSM) historical snapshots dated **at or before T₂**. If an annotator checks live OSM or current Google Earth imagery to see if a cleared plot in Mumbai's 6-month pair eventually became a building, they inject *future temporal leakage* into the ground truth. If T₁ and T₂ Sentinel-2 imagery cannot confirm the cause, label it `"uncertain"`. | - **Add Water/Tidal Differencing (ΔMNDWI).** For Mumbai's coastal reclamation and bridge deck extensions—as well as Delhi's Yamuna river crossings—add the **Modified Normalized Difference Water Index** (\(\text{MNDWI} = \frac{B3 - B11}{B3 + B11}\)). Water-to-impervious transitions (ΔMNDWI < -0.3 paired with ΔNDBI > 0.1) reliably isolate bridge decks and piers without triggering on tidal mudflats. | - **Include Random Negative/Background Candidates.** Do not only annotate polygons generated by your spectral thresholds. Randomly sample 15%–20% of "below-threshold" areas (especially active farmland in Delhi and steep terrain/mangroves in Mumbai) for human review so your dataset captures subtle demolitions or narrow linear roads that raw thresholding misses. |  | 2. 1	# Historical OSM infrastructure reference
2	
3	The downloader in [`../src/utils/historical_osm.py`](../src/utils/historical_osm.py)
4	creates a dated, supporting OpenStreetMap (OSM) reference layer for the Delhi
5	or Mumbai study areas. OSM is not independent ground truth: mapping edits are not construction
6	or demolition dates, and historical OSM may omit deleted or unmapped objects.
7	Verify candidate changes against the Sentinel-2 pair and independent,
8	date-appropriate references where available.
9	
10	## Default area and date
11	
12	The defaults are:
13	
14	- Snapshot: **2023-10-06 23:59:59 UTC** (the state at the end of that UTC day).
15	- Bounding box, in WGS 84 / EPSG:4326 and south, west, north, east order:
16	  `27.9221550832, 77.0320156186, 28.8908178781, 78.1760832868`.
17	- Output directory: `data/labels/osm/delhi_20231006/`.
18	
19	For Delhi, the Sentinel-2 acquisition on 2023-10-06 was at 05:27:01 UTC. The
20	end-of-day OSM snapshot can include edits made after the satellite pass, so it
21	does not represent the exact OSM state at overpass time. OSM edit timestamps
22	also do not establish when physical construction, demolition, or other change
23	occurred.
24	
25	Mumbai uses the T43QBB raster-derived WGS84 footprint
26	`18.8823135663, 72.1523807982, 19.8871534399, 73.1830711420`. By default,
27	the `--city mumbai` command prepares both dated snapshots:
28	
29	- 2022-12-23 23:59:59 UTC, matching the before acquisition.
30	- 2023-05-12 23:59:59 UTC, matching the after acquisition.
31	
32	The Mumbai before and after Sentinel-2 acquisitions occurred at 05:42:41 UTC
33	and 05:36:41 UTC respectively. Neither end-of-day OSM snapshot represents the
34	exact state at the acquisition time.
35	
36	## Run
37	
38	Install the project's pinned dependencies, then run from the project root in
39	PowerShell:
40	
41	```powershell
42	.\venv\Scripts\python.exe src\utils\historical_osm.py
43	```
44	
45	For Mumbai, query both configured snapshots:
46	
47	```powershell

````

 "old_string": "def convert_to_features(\n    data: dict[str, Any], class_id: int, snapshot: str\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    metadata = _element_metadata(data)\n    try:\n        converted = osm2geojson.json2geojson(data, raise_on_failure=True)\n    except Exception as error:\n        raise OverpassError(f\"Could not convert Overpass geometries: {error}\") from error\n\n    features = []\n    for feature in converted.get(\"features\", []):\n        geometry = feature.get(\"geometry\")\n        if not geometry:\n            continue\n        properties = dict(feature.get(\"properties\") or {})\n        identity = _element_identity(properties)\n        if identity is None:\n            continue",
 "new_string": "def convert_to_features(\n    data: dict[str, Any],\n    class_id: int,\n    snapshot: str,\n    skipped: list[dict[str, Any]] | None = None,\n) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:\n    \"\"\"Convert one Overpass response into classified GeoJSON features.\n\n    Elements whose geometry cannot be built (for example, route relations with\n    incomplete members) are dropped individually instead of failing the whole\n    tile. When ``skipped`` is given, one record per dropped element is appended.\n    \"\"\"\n    metadata = _element_metadata(data)\n    try:\n        # Per-element failures are tolerated and reported below; the library's\n        # own logging would otherwise print each failed relation in full.\n        converted = osm2geojson.json2geojson(\n            data, raise_on_failure=False, log_level=\"CRITICAL\"\n        )\n    except Exception as error:\n        raise OverpassError(f\"Could not convert Overpass geometries: {error}\") from error\n\n    features = []\n    for feature in converted.get(\"features\", []):\n        geometry = feature.get(\"geometry\")\n        if not geometry:\n            continue\n        properties = dict(feature.get(\"properties\") or {})\n        identity = _element_identity(properties)\n        if identity is None:\n            continue"
}

**Assistant (claude-opus-5-5) · 2026-11-18T05:52:44.882Z**

I've continued the Mumbai OSM download and fixed a bug that would have quietly lost data. The download is running again in the background and isn't finished yet.

**The bug:** the downloader treated any OSM element whose shape couldn't be built as a failure of its whole tile. In Mumbai, the Metro Line-3 route record triggered this, so every Construction feature in that tile (855 of them) would have been dropped and the tile marked failed. Now only the bad element is dropped, and it's listed in a new `skipped_elements.json` file and counted in the summary file. The library also no longer prints the whole failing record to the log.

**What I checked:**
- All 12 OSM tests pass, including a new one for this case.
- I re-ran the 9 already-downloaded Mumbai responses offline through the fixed code. The 855-feature tile now converts in full, with nothing skipped.
- I haven't run the full project test suite.

**State of the download:**
- The run Copilot started was still going even though it looked stopped. It had saved 9 query responses for 2022-12-23. I stopped it so two runs wouldn't hit the public OSM server at once, and restarted from those saved responses, so nothing gets downloaded twice.
- When it finishes, I'll check each date's summary for `complete` vs `partial` and failed tiles, and give you feature counts per category for both Mumbai dates.

The changes are in [historical_osm.py](src/utils/historical_osm.py), [test_historical_osm.py](tests/test_historical_osm.py) and [HISTORICAL_OSM.md](docs/HISTORICAL_OSM.md). They're not committed yet. I also left `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` alone: it appeared as a new untracked file and I didn't create it.

The Delhi 2023-10-06 download has still never been run; I'll start it after Mumbai if you want.

# Project Summary: Sentinel-2 Man-Made Change Detection

## 1. Objective

This repository develops a CPU-oriented workflow for detecting and reviewing
man-made surface changes from paired Sentinel-2 imagery. The project compares
the Mumbai T43QBB pair and the Delhi T43RGM pair, while keeping candidate
detection, weak reference data, human review, and independent evaluation
separate.

The project does not currently have independent reference labels or verified
change ground truth. It must not claim change detections, model accuracy, or
ground-truth precision from current candidate products.

## 2. Source data and temporal pairs

The supplied Sentinel-2A products support two geographically separate pairs:

| Pair | City | Tile | Before | After | Elapsed time |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| A | Mumbai | T43QBB | 2022-12-23 | 2023-05-12 | 140 days |
| B | Delhi | T43RGM | 2023-10-06 | 2026-10-22 | 1,112 days |

The T43QBB and T43RGM footprints are disjoint. They must not be compared
pixel by pixel as a cross-city temporal pair.

The source products pass the supplied metadata quality reports. Key limitations
are winter-to-spring or dry-season change in Mumbai, long-interval temporary
changes in Delhi, haze, solar-angle differences, shadows, water/tide variation,
vegetation phenology, and residual registration displacement.

## 3. Preprocessing and data preparation

Four Sentinel-2A L1C products were processed through Sen2Cor 2.13.02.
Per-product surface-reflectance stacks and validity masks were generated.
Atmospheric and AOT diagnostics, timing records, and source audit manifests
were added.

The preprocessed bands retained for the six-band wor

Interview Assignment : Man Made Change Detection
Develop and evaluate a reproducible system that detects, localizes and analyses structural man-made changes in satellite imagery acquired at different times. The system must distinguish these changes from natural surface variation and differences caused by image acquisition. Support conclusions with quantitative results and before-and-after evidence.
Inputs and scope
The study imagery will be supplied separately. Assume that all observations come from the same sensor and are not spatially aligned. Do not assume that a pixel in one image represents the same ground location in another. Establish the common coverage and the alignment needed for reliable comparison.
Target changes may include bridge deck extension, new roads, building construction, demolition or other infrastructure development. Define the target classes for the selected imagery. Treat seasonal vegetation variation, water-level changes, erosion and deposition as potential non-target changes. State how agricultural activity is classified and retain an uncertain category where the imagery cannot establish the cause.
Required work
1 Data requirements and execution plan
Audit the supplied imagery and define the required bands, spatial resolution, metadata, image quality, reference labels and computational resources. Explain the proposed workflow, how it handles two or more observations, and any requirements that the supplied data do not meet. Additional data sources must be identified and justified.
2 Preprocessing and registration
Choose and implement the preprocessing needed for the data. Assess geometric alignment, radiometric differences, resolution and observation quality before detection. Document each transformation and measure registration error using stable features. Justify material processing steps through comparisons, and explain how residual misalignment could create false detections.
3 Architecture for onboard and ground execution
Pro

"""Windowed dataset for manually annotated bi-temporal raster patches."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Sequence

import numpy as np
import rasterio
import torch
from rasterio.features import rasterize
from rasterio.warp import transform_geom
from rasterio.windows import Window, transform as window_transform
from torch.utils.data import Dataset


CHANGE_CLASSES = (
    "no_change",
    "construction",
    "demolition",
    "road",
    "bridge",
    "infrastructure_other",
    "uncertain",
)
CLASS_TO_ID = {name: index for index, name in enumerate(CHANGE_CLASSES)}
IGNORE_INDEX = 255
NODATA = -9999.0
REQUIRED_ANNOTATION_PROPERTIES = {
    "change_class",
    "confidence",
    "annotator",
    "notes",
}


def _read_annotations(path: Path, raster_crs) -> list[tuple[dict, int]]:
    try:
        collection = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    except (OSError, json.JSONDecodeError) as error:
        raise ValueError(f"Could not read annotation GeoJSON {path}: {error}") from error
    if collection.get("type") != "FeatureCollection":
        raise ValueError(f"{path} must contain a GeoJSON FeatureCollection")
    features = []
    for index, feature in enumerate(collection.get("features", [])):
        if feature.get("type") != "Feature":
            raise ValueError(f"Annotation feature {index} is not a GeoJSON Feature")
        properties = feature.get("properties") or {}
        missing = REQUIRED_ANNOTATION_PROPERTIES - properties.keys()
        if missing:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Annotation feature {index} is missing properties: "
                f"{', '.join(sorted(missing))}"
            )
        class_name = properties["change_class"]
        if class_name not in CLASS_TO_ID:
            raise ValueError(
                f"Annotation feature {index} has unknown change_class "
                f"{class_name!r}; expected one of {', '.join(CHANGE_CLASSES)}"

def _find_one(directory: Path, pattern: str) -> Path:
    matches = sorted(directory.glob(pattern))
    if len(matches) != 1:
        raise FileNotFoundError(
            f"Expected exactly one file matching {pattern!r} in {directory}, "
            f"found {len(matches)}"
        )
    return matches[0]


def _index(numerator: np.ndarray, denominator: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
    output = np.full(numerator.shape, np.nan, dtype=np.float32)
    np.divide(
        numerator,
        denominator,
        out=output,
        where=np.abs(denominator) > MIN_INDEX_DENOMINATOR,
    )
    return output


def _indices(reflectance: np.ndarray) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:

```
class SiameseUNet(nn.Module):
    """Six-band, seven-class Siamese U-Net; returns per-class logits."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        in_channels: int = len(GROUND_BANDS),
        num_classes: int = len(GROUND_CLASSES),
        widths: Sequence[int] = (32, 64, 128, 256),
    ):
        super().__init__()
        if in_channels <= 0 or num_classes <= 0 or len(widths) < 2:
            raise ValueError("Channels/classes must be positive and need two stages")
        if any(width <= 0 for width in widths):
            raise ValueError("All encoder widths must be positive")
        self.in_channels = in_channels
        self.num_classes = num_classes
        self.encoder = SharedEncoder(in_channels, widths)
        self.bottom = ConvBlock(widths[-1] * 3, widths[-1])
        self.decoder = nn.ModuleList(
            ConvBlock(widths[index + 1] + widths[index] * 3, widths[index])
            for index in range(len(widths) - 2, -1, -1)
        )
        self.classifier = nn.Conv2d(widths[0], num_classes, kernel_size=1)

    @staticmethod
    def _fuse(before: torch.Tensor, after: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        return torch.cat((before, after, torch.abs(after - before)), dim=1)

    def forward(self, before: torch.Tensor, after: torch.Tensor) -> torch.Tensor:
        _validate_pair(before, after, self.in_channels)
        before_features = self.encoder(before)
        after_features = self.encoder(after)
        x = self.bottom(
            self._fuse(before_features[-1], after_features[-1])
        )
        for decoder, index in zip(
            self.decoder, range(len(before_features) - 2, -1, -1)
        ):
            skip = self._fuse(before_features[index], after_features[index])
            x = F.interpolate(x, size=skip.shape[-2:], mode="bilinear", align_corners=False)
            x = decoder(torch.cat((x, skip), dim=1))
        return self.classifier(x)

They are not\nground truth and must never be used as evaluation references; human-reviewed\nwindows are the only evaluation data (assignment section 5).\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport cv2\nimport numpy as np\nimport rasterio\nfrom rasterio.windows import Window\n\nfrom src.training.generate_candidates import NODATA, SCENES, _pair_paths\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nDEFAULT_CONFIG = ROOT / \"configs\" / \"weak_labels.json\"\nDEFAULT_OUTPUT_DIR = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"weak_labels\"\nREVIEW_AREA = {\"Mumbai\": ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"processed\" / \"mumbai_candidate_review_area.tif\"}\n\nSTABLE, GAIN, LOSS, VEGETATION, WATER, AMBIGUOUS, INVALID = 0, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 255\nCODE_NAMES = {\n    STABLE: \"stable\",\n    GAIN: \"built_gain\",\n    LOSS: \"built_loss\",\n    VEGETATION: \"vegetation_change\",\n    WATER: \"water_change\",\n    AMBIGUOUS: \"ambiguous\",\n    INVALID: \"invalid\",\n}\n# Mapping used for model training: natural changes are hard negatives.\nTRAIN_TARGET = {STABLE: 0, VEGETATION: 0, WATER: 0, GAIN: 1, LOSS: 2}\nTRAIN_IGNORE = 255\n\n\ndef _ratio(a: np.ndarray, b: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    out = np.zeros(a.shape, dtype=np.float32)\n    den = a + b\n    np.divide(a - Open\nindex.html in a browser, label windows, then use \"Export labels\" and save the\nfile as data/labels/reviews/review_labels.json.\n\nThe page hides how each window was selected (ranked vs random) and shuffles\norder with a fixed seed so the reviewer's judgement is not anchored to the\ncandidate score.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport random\nimport re\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport cv2\nimport geopandas as gpd\nimport numpy as np\nimport rasterio\nfrom rasterio.windows import Window\n\nfrom src.training.generate_candidates import NODATA, SCENES, _pair_paths\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nOUT = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"review\"\nWEAK = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"weak_labels\"\nSIZE = 256\nSCALE = 2\nSEED = 20261118\n\n\ndef _rgb(stack: np.ndarray, lo: np.ndarray, hi: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    rgb = stack[[2, 1, 0]].astype(np.float32)  # B04, B03, B02\n    rgb = (rgb - lo[:, None, None]) / np.maximum(hi - lo, 1e-6)[:, None, None]\n    rgb = np.clip(rgb, 0, 1) ** (1 / 1.4)\n    img = (rgb.transpose(1, 2, 0)[:, :, ::-1] * 255).astype(np.uint8)  # BGR\n    return cv2.resize(img, None, fx=SCALE, fy=SCALE, interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)\n\n\ndef _overlay(codes: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Transparent PNG outlining weak gain (red) and loss (cyan) components.\"\"\"\n    rgba = np.zeros((SIZE * SCALE, SIZE * SCALE, 4), np.uint8)\n    big = cv2.resize(codes, None, fx=SCALE, fy=SCALE, interpolation=cv2.INTER_NEAREST)\n    for code, colour in ((1, (40, 40, 255, 255)), (2, (255, 230, 0, 255))):\n        mask = (big == code).astype(np.uint8)\n        contours, _ = cv2.findContours(mask, cv2.RETR_EXTERNAL, cv2.CHAIN_APPROX_NONE)\n        cv2.drawContours(rgba, contours, -1, colour, 1)\n    invalid = big == 255\n    rgba[invalid] = (128, 128, 128, 140)\n    return rgba\n\n\ndef build(output: Path = OUT) -> int:\n    chips = output / \"chips\"\n    chips.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)\n    windows = []\n    for scene in SCENES:\n        city = scene[\"city\"].lower()\n        candidates = gpd.read_file(ROOT / \"data\" / \"labels\" / \"candidates\" / scene[\"output\"])\n        before_path, after_path, _ = _pair_paths(scene)\n        with rasterio.open(before_path) as before, rasterio.open(after_path) as after, \\\n                rasterio.open(WEAK / f\"{city}_weak_labels.tif\") as weak:\n            for _, cand in candidates.iterrows():\n                row, col = map(int, re.search(r\"R(\\d+)_C(\\d+)\", cand[\"feature_id\"]).groups())\n                window = Window(col, row, SIZE, SIZE)\n                bef, aft = before.read(window=window), after.read(window=window)\n                codes = weak.read(1, w

 "content": "\"\"\"Freeze whole-block spatial splits with a 2 km training buffer.\n\nEvery 10 km macro-block is assigned to train, val, test, zero_shot or\nexcluded. Training patches may only come from each train block's *core*: the\nblock minus a 2 km buffer around every non-train block, so no training pixel\nlies within 2 km of a validation, test or zero-shot block.\n\nOutputs:\n  data/manifests/spatial_splits.json   block -> split manifest (seeded)\n  data/labels/spatial_splits.gpkg      layers {city}_blocks, {city}_train_cores\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nimport random\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport geopandas as gpd\nfrom shapely.geometry import Point\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nGRIDS = ROOT / \"data\" / \"labels\" / \"metro_label_grids.gpkg\"\nMANIFEST = ROOT / \"data\" / \"manifests\" / \"spatial_splits.json\"\nGPKG = ROOT / \"data\" / \"labels\" / \"spatial_splits.gpkg\"\nSEED = 20261118\nFRACTIONS = {\"train\": 0.50, \"val\": 0.25, \"test\": 0.25}\nBUFFER_M = 2000.0\nMIN_BLOCK_KM2 = 25.0\n# Zero-shot geographic holdouts (lon, lat), verified inside the footprints.\nZERO_SHOT = {\n    \"mumbai\": (\"navi_mumbai_panvel\", (73.08, 19.02)),\n    \"delhi\": (\"noida\", (77.36, 28.55)),\n}\n\n\ndef assign(city: str, blocks: gpd.GeoDataFrame, seed: int = SEED) -> gpd.GeoDataFrame:\n    blocks = blocks.copy()\n    blocks[\"split\"] = \"excluded\"\n    blocks[\"zone_role\"] = \"too_small_or_edge\"\n    name, (lon, lat) = ZERO_SHOT[city]\n    point = gpd.GeoSeries([Point(lon, lat)], crs=4326).to_crs(blocks.crs).iloc[0]\n    zero = blocks.geometry.contains(point)\n    if zero.sum() != 1:\n        raise ValueError(f\"Zero-shot point {name} is not inside exactly one {city} block\")\n    blocks.loc[zero, [\"split\", \"zone_role\"]] = [\"zero_shot\", name]\n\n    eligible = blocks.index[(~zero) & (blocks[\"area_km2\"] >= MIN_BLOCK_KM2)].tolist()\n    rng = random.Random(f\"{seed}-{city}\")\n    rng.shuffle(eligible)\n    n = len(eligible)\n    n_train = round(n * FRACTIONS[\"train\"])\n    n_val = round(n * FRACTIONS[\"val\"])\n    for position, index in enumerate(eligible):\n        split = \"train\" if position < n_train else \"val\" if position < n_train + n_val else \"test\"\n        blocks.loc[index, [\"split\", \"zone_role\"]] = [split, \"conventional\"]\n    return blocks\n\n\ndef train_cores(blocks: gpd.GeoDataFrame) -> gpd.GeoDataFrame:\n    held = blocks[blocks[\"split\"].isin([\"val\", \"test\", \"zero_shot\"])].union_all()\n    exclusion = held.buffer(BUFFER_M)\n    train = blocks[blocks[\"split\"] == \"train\"].copy()\n    train[\"geometry\"] = train.geometry.difference(exclusion)\n    train = train[~train.geometry.is_empty]\n    train[\"core_km2\"] = (train.geometry.area / 1e6).round(2)\n    return train[[\"block_id\", \"split\", \"core_km2\", \"geometry\"]]\n\n\nde

AI assistants were used throughout this assignment. Every chat session tied to this repository is exported in time order below, generated by [`src/utils/export_ai_usage.py`](src/utils/export_ai_usage.py) from the tools' local session logs.

def load_reviews(path: Path) -> list[dict]:
    data = json.loads(path.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
    return data["labels"] if isinstance(data, dict) else data

def small_change_retention(fp: np.ndarray, q: np.ndarray, max_px: int = 10) -> dict:
    """Share of small FP32 change components (<= max_px) still detected by INT8."""
    total = kept = 0
    for a, b in zip(fp, q):
        n, comp, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats((a > 0).astype(np.uint8), connectivity=8)
        for label in range(1, n):
            if stats[label, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA] <= max_px:
                total += 1
                kept += bool((b[comp == label] > 0).any())
    return {"fp32_small_components": total, "retained_by_int8": kept,
            "retention": round(kept / total, 4) if total else None}

254	## 4. Model and deployment design
255	
256	Two CPU-capable PyTorch architectures are implemented in
257	[`../src/models/siamese.py`](../src/models/siamese.py): a shared-weight,
258	six-band Siamese U-Net returning seven per-pixel semantic logits, and a
259	four-band, half-width MobileNetV3-Small Siamese network returning binary
260	change/no-change logits. The ground model uses a custom encoder rather than
261	ResNet/EfficientNet because the inputs are multispectral and no suitable
262	pretrained weights are supplied; the onboard model's first layer is adapted
263	to four channels and initialized without ImageNet weights. Architectural
264	tradeoffs and the class/band order are recorded in
265	[`PHASE_3_ARCHITECTURE.md`](./PHASE_3_ARCHITECTURE.md).

15	
16	The products contain the 13 spectral bands and pass the supplied metadata
17	quality reports. They do not include reference change labels. Reliable
18	comparison therefore still requires pixel-level quality assessment,
19	registration, radiometric assessment, and independently reviewed labels. The
20	development machine has no identified discrete GPU, so the initial workflow is
21	designed for windowed CPU processing. Model results, detections, and
22	quantization measurements will be added only after the corresponding work is
23	implemented and evaluated.
24	
25	## 1. Objective, scope, and change classes
26	
27	The objective is to detect and localize structural man-made changes between
28	observations while distinguishing them from natural surface variation and
29	acquisition effects. Candidate target classes are:
30	
31	- construction / new building;
32	- demolition / removed building;
33	- new or extended road;
34	- bridge or other transport infrastructure change;
35	- other infrastructure change; and
36	- uncertain, when the imagery cannot establish the cause or class.
37	
38	Seasonal vegetation, agriculture, water-level change, erosion/deposition,
39	cloud/shadow, and registration or illumination artifacts are not positive
40	structural-change labels. Agricultural activity is treated as non-targe

**Assistant (claude-opus-5-5) · 2026-11-18T06:43:53.667Z**

The pipeline is built and tested. Two things now gate the results: the hyperparameter search finishing and your labels from the review page.

**Built since your last message (all 49 tests pass):**
- **Weak labels** for both cities, generated automatically. They mark built-up change while ignoring 1-pixel misalignment, and set haze, dry-season vegetation and tiny blobs aside as ambiguous.
- **Spatial splits:** each city is divided into 10 km blocks: 50% training, 25% validation, 25% test, plus a never-touched test block in Navi Mumbai and one in Noida. Training data stays at least 2 km from any test block.
- **Review page** (the HTML): blind, with evaluation windows first.
- **Training and hyperparameter search:** running in the background, 2 of 9 runs done. Best so far is plain cross-entropy at learning rate 3e-3, with weak-validation F1 0.488. It should finish around 14:10.
- **Evaluation:** a window is flagged as changed when the model's change pixels pass a threshold set on validation windows only. The rule-based labels are scored the same way as a baseline, and natural false alarms are measured.
- **Quantization:** standard INT8 quantization was slower than FP32. Quantizing only the convolutions and keeping the before/after difference step in full precision made it about 26% faster than FP32 at 4× smaller. Small-change retention still needs checking on the trained models.
- **Change maps, detection register and evidence gallery:** code written; the maps and register were smoke-tested on a small Delhi area.
- **`AI_USAGE.md`:** full transcripts of both Copilot sessions and this Claude session, with your email redacted.
- **Docs:** new [DECISION_LOG.md](docs/DECISION_LOG.md) (12 decisions) and a root [README.md](README.md) with reproduction steps.

**What I need from you:**
1. **Labels.** Work through the review page; the 150 evaluation windows matter most. Export to `data/labels/reviews/review_labels.json`. Every reported metric depends on this.
2. **OSM scope.** The Mumbai download is stuck on the huge buildings/infrastructure queries: about 2 new responses an hour, so it won't finish today. Delhi is queued behind it. Should I drop the buildings/infrastructure category, so the construction, demolition, road and bridge categories finish in time? Or accept OSM as incomplete and say so in the report?
3. **Commits.** Nothing new is committed yet, and the assignment rewards meaningful history. Can I commit in logical chunks: the OSM fix, the label pipeline, training and evaluation, then docs?

Once the search finishes and your labels are in, I'll evaluate, quantize the chosen models, run the full-tile maps and register, build the evidence gallery, and finish the technical report.

2. Key Technical Concepts:
   - Sentinel-2 L2A via Sen2Cor; 6-band stack in this order: B02, B03, B04, B08, B11, B12. Float reflectance 0–1, nodata -9999, 10980×10980 grids, EPSG:32643.
   - Indices: NDVI = (B08−B04)/(B08+B04); NDBI = (B11−B08)/(B11+B08); MNDWI = (B03−B11)/(B03+B11).
   - Registration-tolerant differencing: NDBI change minus the 3×3 neighbourhood maximum (cv2.dilate). Residual registration error is about 1 px.
   - Haze rule: 31×31 mean blue increase ≥ 0.035 marks pixels ambiguous.
   - Weak-label codes: 0 stable, 1 gain, 2 loss, 3 vegetation, 4 water, 5 ambiguous, 255 invalid.
     - Training mapping: {0, 3, 4} → 0, 1 → 1, 2 → 2; others ignored (255).
   - Spatial splits: whole 10 km blocks, 50/25/25 per city, seed 20261118. Zero-shot blocks: Mumbai `T43QBB_E0290_N2100` (Navi Mumbai/Panvel), Delhi `T43RGM_E0730_N3160` (Noida). Train cores are the train blocks minus a 2 km buffer around every non-train block.
   - Blind review page: offline HTML with keyboard shortcuts and localStorage autosave. Exports to `data/labels/reviews/review_labels.json`. Selection type is hidden, order is shuffled with a seed, and evaluation windows (priority 0) come first.
   - Models:
     - Ground: SiameseUNet, 3 classes, widths (16, 32, 64, 128), 1,175,571 parameters.
     - Onboard: SiameseMobileNetV3Small, 4 bands, binary, 279,274 parameters.
   - Training: CE / weighted CE / weighted CE + Dice with ignore_index 255; rot90/flip plus per-date, per-band ±5% gain jitter; AdamW with OneCycleLR; selection by weak-validation change F1 (a proxy only).
   - Evaluation unit: the reviewed window. A window is flagged when change pixels ≥ k.
     - k is chosen on validation windows from `K_GRID = [1, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128, 256, 512, 1024]`.
     - POSITIVE = {construction, demolition, road, bridge, infrastructure_other}
     - NEGATIVE = {no_change, natural_vegetation, natural_water, acquisition_artifact}
     - Uncertain windows are reported separately and never scored.
     - Bootstrap CIs; the rule baseline is scored identically.
   - Quantization: ONNX Runtime static PTQ. Default variant `qdq_conv_u8` (QDQ, Conv-only, UINT8 activations, per-channel INT8 weights, MinMax), calibrated on 64 train patches zero-padded to 256. Normalization, softmax, and the Sub/Abs temporal-difference ops stay in FP32.
   - Detection type rules:
     - loss → demolition
     - gain with ≥ 50% T1 water (MNDWI > 0.2) → bridge_or_reclamation
     - elongated gain (long/short side ≥ 4 and width ≤ 3 px) → road
     - other gain → construction
     - Area uncertainty = boundary pixels × 100 m².
     - Confidence tiers: high ≥ 0.8, medium ≥ 0.65, otherwise low.
     - Review status: accepted / accepted_type_disputed / rejected_<class> / uncertain / unreviewed.

 "new_string": "def ranking(score: np.ndarray, truth: np.ndarray) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Average precision and AUROC of a window score; ties share ranks.\"\"\"\n    pos, neg = int(truth.sum()), int((~truth).sum())\n    out = {\"n\": int(len(truth)), \"positives\": pos, \"prevalence\": round(pos / len(truth), 4) if len(truth) else None}\n    if not pos or not neg:\n        return {**out, \"average_precision\": None, \"auroc\": None}\n    order = np.argsort(-score, kind=\"stable\")\n    s, t = score[order], truth[order]\n    tp = np.cumsum(t)\n    # Evaluate precision only at the last index of each tied score group.\n    last = np.r_[s[1:] != s[:-1], True]\n    prec = tp[last] / (np.flatnonzero(last) + 1)\n    rec_gain = np.diff(np.r_[0, tp[last]]) / pos\n    ap = float((prec * rec_gain).sum())\n    ranks = np.empty(len(score))\n    sorted_s = np.sort(score)\n    lo = np.searchsorted(sorted_s, score, \"left\")\n    hi = np.searchsorted(sorted_s, score, \"right\")\n    ranks = (lo + hi + 1) / 2.0\n    auroc = float((ranks[truth].sum() - pos * (pos + 1) / 2) / (pos * neg))\n    return {**out, \"average_precision\": round(ap, 4), \"auroc\": round(auroc, 4)}\n\n\ndef score_method("
}

 "content": "\"\"\"Human-corrected training patches from reviewed windows in TRAIN blocks only.\n\nThe blind review showed that the weak-label rules mark large agricultural and\nhaze-affected areas as built-up change. Reviewed windows that lie in train\nblocks may be used for training without touching validation, test or\nzero-shot labels:\n\n- reviewed natural_vegetation / natural_water / acquisition_artifact /\n  no_change -> every valid pixel becomes \"no change\" (hard negatives);\n- reviewed construction / demolition / road / bridge / infrastructure_other\n  -> the weak gain/loss outline is kept (other pixels as in the weak labels);\n- uncertain windows are skipped.\n\nEach 256 px window gives nine 96 px crops (stride 80). Output:\noutputs/patches/reviewed_train.npz and reviewed_train.json.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom src.evaluation.evaluate import NEGATIVE, POSITIVE, attach_splits\nfrom src.evaluation.windows import load_cache, load_reviews\nfrom src.training.weak_labels import TRAIN_IGNORE, TRAIN_TARGET\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nOUT = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"patches\"\nPATCH = 96\nOFFSETS = (0, 80, 160)\n\n\ndef weak_to_target(weak: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    y = np.full(weak.shape, TRAIN_IGNORE, np.uint8)\n    for code, target in TRAIN_TARGET.items():\n        y[weak == code] = target\n    return y\n\n\ndef corrected_target(weak: np.ndarray, valid: np.ndarray, human_class: str) -> np.ndarray:\n    if human_class in NEGATIVE:\n        y = np.zeros(weak.shape, np.uint8)\n    else:\n        y = weak_to_target(weak)\n    y[~valid] = TRAIN_IGNORE\n    return y\n\n\ndef build(reviews_path: Path = ROOT / \"data\" / \"labels\" / \"reviews\" / \"review_labels.json\") -> dict:\n    reviews = attach_splits(load_reviews(reviews_path))\n    cache = load_cache()\n    index = {fid: i for i, fid in enumerate(cache[\"ids\"])}\n    xs, ys, used = [], [], {}\n    for r in reviews:\n        cls = r[\"change_class\"]\n        if r[\"split\"] != \"train\" or cls not in POSITIVE | NEGATIVE:\n            continue\n        i = index[r[\"feature_id\"]]\n        y = corrected_target(cache[\"weak\"][i], cache[\"valid\"][i], cls)\n        for oy in OFFSETS:\n            for ox in  "new_string": "    for r in reviews:\n        r[\"block\"] = blocks[r[\"feature_id\"]]\n        r[\"split\"] = manifest[r[\"city\"].lower()][\"blocks\"].get(r[\"block\"], \"excluded\")\n    return reviews\n\n\ndef select_k(change: np.ndarray, truth: np.ndarray) -> int:\n    best_k, best_f1 = K_GRID[-1], -1.0\n    for k in K_GRID:\n        f1 = prf(change >= k, truth)[\"f1\"] if len(truth) else 0.0\n        if f1 > best_f1:  # ties keep the smaller k (higher sensitivity)\n            best_k, best_f1 = k, f1\n    return best_k\n\n\ndef grouped_cv(change: np.ndarray, truth: np.ndarray, groups: np.ndarray,\n               cls: np.ndarray, folds: int = 5) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Out-of-fold window decisions: k chosen on the other folds' blocks.\n\n    Uses all held-out (val + test + zero-shot) windows, whose blocks no model\n    was trained on, instead of the 2 validation positives alone.\n    \"\"\"\n    unique = np.array(sorted(set(groups)))\n    rng = np.random.default_rng(SEED)\n    fold_of_block = dict(zip(rng.permutation(unique), np.arange(len(unique)) % folds))\n    fold = np.array([fold_of_block[g] for g in groups])\n    pred = np.zeros(len(truth), bool)\n    ks = []\n    for f in range(folds):\n        k = select_k(change[fold != f], truth[fold != f])\n        ks.append(k)\n        pred[fold == f] = change[fold == f] >= k\n    natural = np.isin(cls, list(NATURAL))\n    return {**prf(pred, truth), \"f1_95ci\": bootstrap_f1(pred, truth), \"folds\": folds,\n            \"blocks\": int(len(unique)), \"k_per_fold\": ks,\n            \"natural_false_alarm_rate\": round(float(pred[natural].mean()), 4) if natural.any() else None}"
}

1	# Man-Made Change Detection from Sentinel-2 Imagery
2	
3	**Technical report — working document**  
4	**Status:** Requirements and data audit completed; implementation, experiments,
5	and results are in progress. This report distinguishes verified facts from
6	proposed methods. No change detections or model-performance results are claimed
7	yet.
8	
9	## Executive summary
10	
11	The supplied data comprise four Sentinel-2A Level-1C products. They support two
12	same-tile temporal comparisons: T43QBB from December 2022 to May 2023, and
13	T43RGM from October 2023 to October 2026. The two tiles are geographically
14	disjoint and must not be compared pixel by pixel.
15	
16	The products contain the 13 spectral bands and pass the supplied metadata
17	quality reports. They do not include reference change labels. Reliable
18	comparison therefore still requires pixel-level quality assessment,
19	registration, radiometric assessment, and independently reviewed labels. The
20	development machine has no identified discrete GPU, so the initial workflow is
21	designed for windowed CPU processing. Model results, detections, and
22	quantization measurements will be added only after the corresponding work is
23	implemented and evaluated.
24	
25	## 1. Objective, scope, and change classes
26	
27	The objective is to detect and localize structural man-made changes between
28	observations while distinguishing them from natural surface variation and
29	acquisition effects. Candidate target classes are:
30	
31	- construction / new building;
32	- demolition / removed building;
33	- new or extended road;
34	- bridge or other transport infrastructure change;
35	- other infrastructure change; and
36	- uncertain, when the imagery cannot establish the cause or class.
37	
38	Seasonal vegetation, agriculture, water-level change, erosion/deposition,
39	cloud/shadow, and registration or illumination artifacts are not positive
40	structural-change labels. Agricultural activity is treated as non-targe
... [truncated 526 characters]
```

</details>

250	The implementation must be windowed: one full-tile 10 m float32 band is about
251	1.93 GB decimal, exceeding available free RAM on the inspected development
252	machine. Full-tile multi-band arrays are not a safe processing strategy here.
253	
254	## 4. Model and deployment design
255	
256	Two CPU-capable PyTorch architectures are implemented in
257	[`../src/models/siamese.py`](../src/models/siamese.py): a shared-weight,
258	six-band Siamese U-Net returning seven per-pixel semantic logits, and a
259	four-band, half-width MobileNetV3-Small Siamese network returning binary
260	change/no-change logits. The ground model uses a custom encoder rather than
261	ResNet/EfficientNet because the inputs are multispectral and no suitable
262	pretrained weights are supplied; the onboard model's first layer is adapted
263	to four channels and initialized without ImageNet weights. Architectural
264	tradeoffs and the class/band order are recorded in
265	[`PHASE_3_ARCHITECTURE.md`](./PHASE_3_ARCHITECTURE.md).
266	
267	On the inspected CPU-only development machine, batch-1 256×256 synthetic
268	patch profiling with four CPU threads measured 4,696,743 parameters,
269	40.35 GFLOPs, 0.246 s first-forward latency, and 455.3 MiB sampled process RSS
270	for the ground model; the onboard model measured 279,274 parameters,
271	0.118 GFLOPs, 0.025 s, and 241.3 MiB sampled process RSS. These are proxy
272	measurements, not target-device benchmarks or sustained throughput; the
273	onboard <5M parameter limit is met, but its power, memory behavior, and
274	latency remain unverified on an actual edge device. Neither model has been
275	trained, calibrated, or evaluated for accuracy.
276	
277	Observed development system: Windows 11; AMD Ryzen 5 7530U, 6 cores / 12
278	logical processors; 15.3 GiB RAM (about 4.6 GiB free when inspected); AMD
279	integrated Radeon graphics; no discrete CUDA-capable GPU identified; about
280	95.8 GiB free on C:. The initial training/inference baseline is CPU-only.

... [truncated 9876 characters]

# Phase 3 — Model architecture

## Implemented models

Both models consume aligned before/after image patches in PyTorch NCHW layout
and return full-resolution **logits**. Apply softmax (ground) or two-class
softmax (onboard) only when probabilities are needed. Neither model includes
training, tiling, geospatial I/O, or a trained checkpoint.

| Model | Input bands per date | Encoder/fusion | Output |
|---|---|---|---|
| Ground `SiameseUNet` | B02, B03, B04, B08, B11, B12 | Shared four-stage convolutional encoder; concatenate both features and their absolute difference at each scale; U-Net decoder | 7 classes: no-change, construction, demolition, road, bridge, infrastructure-other, uncertain |
----
# Phase 4 — Training data and supervised training

## Status and blocker

Phase 4 has started with a windowed annotated-patch dataset reader in
[`../src/training/dataset.py`](../src/training/dataset.py). There are no
human-reviewed annotations in `data/labels/`, so training, hyperparameter
search, and accuracy claims are intentionally blocked. Spectral/index
thresholds and unreviewed candidate detections must not be used as ground
truth. Do not silently label every unannotated pixel as no-change.

Phase 2 also records unresolved haze in the later T43RGM scene and approximate
one-pixel residual displacement observed by the user in Delhi and Mumbai.
Exclude suspect haze and registration-artifact areas from trusted labels, or
annotate them as `uncertain` when their status cannot be resolved.
----
# Phase 1 — Data requirements and audit

Audit of the supplied Sentinel-2 products for man-made change detection.
Product metadata, tile metadata, and the supplied quality reports were inspected
locally. Archive checksums and byte sizes are recorded in
[`data/manifests/sentinel2_products.csv`](../data/manifests/sentinel2_products.csv).

## 1.1 Product audit

All four products are Sentinel-2A Level-1C (top-of-atmosphere reflectance) in
descending o

{
 "file_path": "c:\\Users\\Satleo Labs-O\\Downloads\\Interview task\\src\\evaluation\\window_filter.py",
 "content": "\"\"\"Second-stage window filter learned from human reviews in TRAIN blocks only.\n\nThe per-pixel models flag nearly every reviewed window, because agricultural,\nwater and haze changes produce more \"built-up\" pixels than real construction\n(D-013, D-014). This stage scores a window from features of the model's change\nmap and the spectral context of the changed pixels, using a small logistic\nregression with fixed settings (no tuning):\n\n- log change pixels, log largest component, component count, share of change\n  in components <= 50 px (compactness);\n- mean T1 NDVI, mean dNDVI, mean dNDBI and mean dMNDWI over changed pixels;\n- regional blue increase (haze proxy) over the window.\n\nFit on decided reviewed windows in train blocks; decision threshold chosen on\nthe same train windows (max F1); evaluated once on validation + test +\nzero-shot windows. Writes outputs/evaluation/window_filter.json.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport cv2\nimport numpy as np\nfrom sklearn.linear_model import LogisticRegression\nfrom sklearn.pipeline import make_pipeline\nfrom sklearn.preprocessing import StandardScaler\n\nfrom src.evaluation.evaluate import (NATURAL, NEGATIVE, OUT, POSITIVE, REVIEWS, attach_splits,\n                                     bootstrap_f1, prf, ranking)\nfrom src.evaluation.windows import (change_map, load_cache, load_reviews, predict_windows,\n                                    torch_predictor)\n\nFEATURES = [\"log_change_px\", \"log_largest_px\", \"components\", \"compact_share\",\n            \"t1_ndvi\", \"d_ndvi\", \"d_ndbi\", \"d_mndwi\", \"blue_increase\"]\nEPS = 1e-6\n\n\ndef _nd(a, b):\n    return (a - b) / (a + b + EPS)\n\n\ndef window_features(x: np.ndarray, valid: np.ndarray, cmap: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"x: (2, 6, H, W) reflectance; cmap: (H, W) 0/1/2 change classes.\"\"\"\n    b, a = x[0].astype(np.float32), x[1].astype(np.float32)\n    ndvi_b, ndvi_a = _nd(b[3], b[2]), _nd(a[3], a[2])\n    ndbi_b, ndbi_a = _nd(b[4], b[3]), _nd(a[4], a[3])\n    mndwi_b, mndwi_a = _nd(b[1], b[4]), _nd(a[1], a[4])\n    change = cmap > 0\n    n, _, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(change.astype(np.uint8), connectivity=8)\n    areas = stats[1:, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA] if n > 1 else np.array([0])\n    total = int(change.sum())\n    m = change if total else valid\n\n    def mean(v):\n        return float(v[m].mean()) if m.any() else 0.0\n\n    return np.array([\n        np.log1p(total), np.log1p(areas.max()), float(n - 1),\n        float(areas[areas <= 50].sum() / total) if total else 0.0,\n        mean(ndvi_b), mean(ndvi_a - ndvi_b), mean(ndbi_a - ndbi_b), mean(mndwi_a - mndwi_b),\n        float((a[0] - b[0])[valid].mean()) if valid.any() else 0.0,\n    ], np.float32)\n\n\ndef best_threshold(score: np.ndarray, truth: np.ndarray
... [truncated 3346 characters]

{
 "file_path": "c:\\Users\\Satleo Labs-O\\Downloads\\Interview task\\src\\inference\\context_score.py",
 "content": "\"\"\"Tile-wide second-stage context score (window filter) for a change map.\n\nApplies the logistic window filter fitted on train-block human reviews\n(src/evaluation/window_filter.py -> <run>/window_filter.json) to every\nnon-overlapping 256 x 256 px window of a full-tile change map. Output:\noutputs/maps/<city>_<tag>_context.tif, one float32 value per window (P of\nman-made change in that 2.56 km window; -1 where the window has no valid\npixels), on a 2,560 m grid aligned with the 10 m grid.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport argparse\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\nimport rasterio\nfrom rasterio.windows import Window\n\nfrom src.evaluation.window_filter import window_features\nfrom src.training.generate_candidates import NODATA, SCENES, _pair_paths\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nMAPS = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"maps\"\nSIZE = 256\n\n\ndef load_filter(path: Path) -> dict:\n    return json.loads(path.read_text(encoding=\"utf-8\"))\n\n\ndef score(features: np.ndarray, params: dict) -> float:\n    z = (features - np.array(params[\"mean\"])) / np.array(params[\"scale\"])\n    return float(1 / (1 + np.exp(-(z @ np.array(params[\"coef\"]) + params[\"intercept\"]))))\n\n\ndef build(city: str, tag: str, params: dict, out: Path = MAPS) -> Path:\n    scene = next(s for s in SCENES if s[\"city\"].lower() == city)\n    before_path, after_path, mask_path = _pair_paths(scene)\n    cls_path = out / f\"{city}_{tag}_change_class.tif\"\n    with rasterio.open(before_path) as b, rasterio.open(after_path) as a, \\\n            rasterio.open(mask_path) as m, rasterio.open(cls_path) as c:\n        if c.width != b.width or c.height != b.height:\n            raise ValueError(\"context score needs a full-tile change map\")\n        rows, cols = int(np.ceil(b.height / SIZE)), int(np.ceil(b.width / SIZE))\n        grid = np.full((rows, cols), -1.0, np.float32)\n        for r in range(rows):\n            for q in range(cols):\n                win = Window(q * SIZE, r * SIZE, min(SIZE, b.width - q * SIZE), min(SIZE, b.height - r * SIZE))\n                valid = m.read(1, window=win) == 1\n                if not valid.any():\n                    continue\n                bef, aft = b.read(window=win), a.read(window=win)\n                valid &= np.all(bef != NODATA, 0) & np.all(aft != NODATA, 0)\n                if not valid.any():\n                    continue\n                bef[:, ~valid] = 0\n                aft[:, ~valid] = 0\n                cmap = c.read(1, window=win)\n                cmap[(cmap == 255) | ~valid] = 0\n                grid[r, q] = score(window_features(np.stack([bef, aft]), valid, cmap), params)\n        profile = {\"driver\": \"GTiff\", \"width\": cols, \"height\": rows, \"count\": 1, \"dtype\": \"float32\",\n                   \"crs\": b.crs, \"nodata\": -1.0,\n    
... [truncated 1247 characters]

 "new_string": "    # Second-stage context score of the enclosing 2.56 km window (if computed).\n    ctx_path = out / f\"{city}_{tag}_context.tif\"\n    if ctx_path.exists():\n        with rasterio.open(ctx_path) as ctx:\n            grid, thr = ctx.read(1), float(ctx.tags().get(\"threshold\", \"nan\"))\n            cents = gdf.geometry.centroid\n            rr, cc = rasterio.transform.rowcol(ctx.transform, cents.x.values, cents.y.values)\n            rr = np.clip(np.asarray(rr), 0, grid.shape[0] - 1)\n            cc = np.clip(np.asarray(cc), 0, grid.shape[1] - 1)\n            gdf[\"context_score\"] = np.round(grid[rr, cc], 4)\n            gdf[\"context_flag\"] = gdf[\"context_score\"] >= thr\n            # Priority for analysts: context first, then pixel confidence.\n            gdf[\"priority_score\"] = np.round(gdf[\"context_score\"] * gdf[\"confidence\"], 4)\n    centroids = gdf.geometry.centroid.to_crs(4326)"

50	## 2. Data audit and requirements
51	
52	The detailed metadata record and audit method are in
53	[`PHASE_1_DATA_AUDIT.md`](./PHASE_1_DATA_AUDIT.md). Archive byte sizes and
54	SHA-256 checksums are recorded in
55	[`../data/manifests/sentinel2_products.csv`](../data/manifests/sentinel2_products.csv).
56	
57	| Pair | Before → after | Tile / orbit | Interval | Main interpretation risk |
58	|---|---|---|---:|---|
59	| A | 2022-12-23 → 2023-05-12 | T43QBB / 5 | 140 days | Winter-to-spring vegetation and illumination change |
60	| B | 2023-10-06 → 2026-10-22 | T43RGM / 105 | 1,112 days | Long interval; temporary changes and intervening events are unobserved |
61	
62	All products are Sentinel-2A L1C, descending orbit, in EPSG:32643. Baselines
63	are 05.10 except the 2026 product (05.13). Each contains 14 non-empty JP2
64	images (13 bands plus TCI), a `MSK_CLASSI_B00.jp2` classification mask, and
65	nine supplied quality reports with `PASSED` status. Product-level cloudy-pixel
66	assessments range from 0% to 0.0077%; these are metadata assessments, not
67	measured clear-area fractions.
68	
69	The metadata gives 10 m, 20 m, and 60 m grids. The 10 m working grid is chosen
70	to retain the finest available output grid for building/road analysis; 20 m and
71	60 m bands remain intrinsically coarser after resampling. Use bilinear

81	
82	Useful indices include NDVI = (B08 − B04)/(B08 + B04), NDWI = (B03 −

 "content": "### How the preprocessing limits were handled downstream\n\n- **~1 px residual misregistration:** the default registered rasters (not\n  the 15 m comparison) are used throughout. Model and deployment design\n\nTwo Siamese (shared-weight) architectures are implemented in\n[`../src/models/siamese.py`](../src/models/siamese.py) and trained from\nscratch (no pretrained weights exist for these six-band inputs):\n\n| | Ground model | Onboard model |\n|---|---|---|\n| Architecture | Siamese U-Net, widths (16, 32, 64, 128); features of both dates and their absolute difference fused at each scale | Siamese MobileNetV3-Small (width 0.5), difference fusion, light decoder |\n| Input | B02, B03, B04, B08, B11, B12 (10 m; 20 m bands resampled) | B02, B03, B04, B08 (native 10 m only) |\n| Output | 3 classes: no change, built-up gain, built-up loss | change / no change |\n| Parameters | 1,175,571 | 279,274 (< 5 M limit) |\n| FP32 / INT8 size | 4.50 / 1.21 MiB | 1.13 / 0.73 MiB |\n| Latency per 256 px window, 1 thread (FP32 / INT8) | 124 / 83 ms | 11 / 12 ms |\n\nWidth was halved from the Phase 3 design (4.7 M parameters) to fit CPU\ntraining on the development machine (AMD Ryzen 5 7530U, 6 cores / 12\nthreads, 15.3 GiB RAM with ~4 GiB free, no GPU). The 7-class head was\nreplaced with 3 classes because spectral weak labels cannot separate road,\nbridge and other infrastructure; types are assigned per detection by\nrecorded rules (D-006).\n\n**Deployment split.** Onboard: the 4-band binary model screens each pair\nand downlinks only change-candidate tiles (on this CPU about 6 ms per 256 px\nwindow with 4 threads, i.e. roughly 15 s per 110 km tile; not measured on\nflight hardware). Ground: the 6-band model, the context filter and the\nregister run on full pairs (~5 min per tile per city on the development\nCPU), and analysts review detections in priority order. **Assistant (claude-opus-5-5) · 2026-11-18T11:02:15.045Z**

The remaining work is done and committed, and it all ran on your laptop in roughly an hour and a half. The main finding is negative, though: the models cannot tell farmland from construction, and the report says so.

**Run times on your machine:**

| Step | Time |
|---|---|
| Evaluating all models on the 306 windows | about 2 min per run |
| One extra training run | 6 min |
| Quantization | 4.5 min |
| Full-tile maps (2,500 tiles per city) | 2.5–5 min per city |
| Detection register | 2–4 min |

**Results:**
- **Your labels:** only 24 of the 306 windows (8%) are real man-made change, and only 2 of those are in validation.
- **Pixel models:** they copied the automatic labels' mistake. Farmland, water and haze produce more detected change than real construction does. Ranking windows by amount of change does worse than random (AUROC 0.36). Every model, including the onboard one, is about as good as flagging every window. The selected ground model scores test F1 0.20 against 0.17 for the rules, and that gap is within noise.
- **Retraining with your labels didn't help.** I fine-tuned on your labelled windows from the training areas only, and the results barely changed.
- **What does work:** a small second-stage window filter, trained only on your training-area labels and tested once on held-out windows.
  - It ranks held-out windows well: AUROC 0.76, confidence interval 0.64–0.86.
  - Natural false alarms fall from 74% to 5.5%.
  - Its threshold is too strict, catching only 1 of the 10 held-out positives, so I use it to rank detections rather than delete them.
- **Detection register:** 86,982 detections across both cities, mostly farmland false alarms. 831 are flagged as high priority, and 47% of those sit in windows you marked as real change, against 3.5% elsewhere. The evidence panels show the problem directly: even in correctly flagged windows, many outlines are bright bare fields.
- **Quantization (INT8):** the ground model drops from 4.5 to 1.2 MiB and runs 1.5× faster with the same F1. It misses 15% of the smallest changes, so the register uses the original FP32 model. INT8 gives no speed-up for the onboard model on this CPU.

**On your split idea:** temporal n-fold isn't possible, because each city has only one before/after pair. The scarce data is your labelled positives, not training data. So I kept the frozen split and added 5-fold cross-validation grouped by 10 km block over all held-out windows. It confirmed the result above with no retraining. Full retraining per fold would take about 1.5 hours and wouldn't add any labelled positives.

**Docs:** every section is updated. The technical report's summary and sections 4–9 are rewritten with the measured numbers, sections 2–3 note how haze and misregistration were handled, and OSM is marked as dropped. There's a new [PHASE_5_EVALUATION.md](docs/PHASE_5_EVALUATION.md), status notes on the Phase 1–4 docs, decisions D-013 to D-016 in the log, and a README demo that I ran successfully.

**Commits:** six more on `feat/weak-label-pipeline` (code, labels and results, models, maps and register, docs, AI record). The full GeoPackages and probability maps (about 130 MB) are too big for git and are rebuilt by the README commands.

Still up to you:
- Merging to `main`: `git switch main && git merge --ff-only feat/weak-label-pipeline`.
- `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` is still uncommitted, and its OSM section is out of date.
- The AI usage record was exported just before this reply. Re-run `src.utils.export_ai_usage` at the very end to include it.

1	# Project Summary: Sentinel-2 Man-Made Change Detection
2	
3	## 1. Objective
4	
5	This repository develops a CPU-oriented workflow for detecting and reviewing
6	man-made surface changes from paired Sentinel-2 imagery. The project compares
7	the Mumbai T43QBB pair and the Delhi T43RGM pair, while keeping candidate
8	detection, weak reference data, human review, and independent evaluation
9	separate.
10	
11	The project does not currently have independent reference labels or verified
12	change ground truth. It must not claim change detections, model accuracy, or
13	ground-truth precision from current candidate products.
14	
15	## 2. Source data and temporal pairs
16	
17	The supplied Sentinel-2A products support two geographically separate pairs:
18	
19	| Pair | City | Tile | Before | After | Elapsed time |
20	|---|---|---|---|---|---|
21	| A | Mumbai | T43QBB | 2022-12-23 | 2023-05-12 | 140 days |
22	| B | Delhi | T43RGM | 2023-10-06 | 2026-10-22 | 1,112 days |
23	
24	The T43QBB and T43RGM footprints are disjoint. They must not be compared
25	pixel by pixel as a cross-city temporal pair.
26	
27	The source products pass the supplied metadata quality reports. Key limitations
28	are winter-to-spring or dry-season change in Mumbai, long-interval temporary
29	changes in Delhi, haze, solar-angle differences, shadows, water/tide variation,
30	vegetation phenology, and residual registration displacement.
31	
32	## 3. Preprocessing and data preparation
33	
34	Four Sentinel-2A L1C products were processed through Sen2Cor 2.13.02.
35	Per-product surface-reflectance stacks and validity masks were generated.
36	Atmospheric and AOT diagnostics, timing records, and source audit manifests
37	were added.
38	
39	The preprocessed bands retained for the six-band working stack are:
40	
41	- B02, B03, B04, B08, B11, and B12
42	
43	The six-band reflectance is 10 m and is intended for windowed change-review
44	experimentation. Common-valid masks are intersections of valid-pixel and SCL

Interview Assignment : Man Made Change Detection
Develop and evaluate a reproducible system that detects, localizes and analyses structural man-made changes in satellite imagery acquired at different times. The system must distinguish these changes from natural surface variation and differences caused by image acquisition. Support conclusions with quantitative results and before-and-after evidence.
Inputs and scope
The study imagery will be supplied separately. Assume that all observations come from the same sensor and are not spatially aligned. Do not assume that a pixel in one image represents the same ground location in another. Establish the common coverage and the alignment needed for reliable comparison.
Target changes may include bridge deck extension, new roads, building construction, demolition or other infrastructure development. Define the target classes for the selected imagery. Treat seasonal vegetation variation, water-level changes, erosion and deposition as potential non-target changes. State how agricultural activity is classified and retain an uncertain category where the imagery cannot establish the cause.
Required work
1 Data requirements and execution plan
Audit the supplied imagery and define the required bands, spatial resolution, metadata, image quality, reference labels and computational resources. 2 Preprocessing and registration
Choose and implement the preprocessing needed for the data. Assess geometric alignment, radiometric differences, resolution and observation quality before detection.  "content": "# Project Summary: Sentinel-2 Man-Made Change Detection\n\nStatus: **complete for submission (2026-11-18).** This page is a short\noverview; the full account is in the [technical report](docs/TECHNICAL_REPORT.md),\n[Phase 5 results](docs/PHASE_5_EVALUATION.md), the\n[decision log](docs/DECISION_LOG.md) and the [README](README.md) (reproduction\nsteps and a demo).\n\n## 1. Objective\n\nDetect, localise and analyse structural man-made change between two\nSentinel-2 dates, and separate it from natural variation (vegetation,\nagriculture, water) and acquisition effects (haze, cloud, misregistration).\nThe project is CPU-only and has separate onboard and ground models.\n\n## 2. Data\n\n| Pair | City | Tile | Before → after | Interval |\n|---|---|---|---|---:|\n| A | Mumbai | T43QBB | 2022-12-23 → 2023-05-12 | 140 days |\n| B | Delhi | T43RGM | 2023-10-06 → 2026-10-22 | 1,112 days |\n\nThe two tiles are disjoint and are never compared with each other. Each city\nhas only one pair, so held-out areas test geographic, not temporal,\ngeneralisation.\n\n## 3. Preprocessing and registration\n\n- Sen2Cor 2.13.02 converts L1C to L2A surface reflectance. The six-band\n  10 m stack (B02, B03, B04, B08, B11, B12) uses an SCL-based validity mask.\n- The later date is registered to the earlier one with ORB/RANSAC. Visual\n  checks show about one pixel of residual shift, which is modelled\n  downstream (3×3 tolerance in the labelling rules; ± one boundary pixel on\n  areas).\n- The 2026 Delhi scene has regional haze that SCL misses. It is handled by\n  a regional haze rule and the review class `acquisition_artifact`.\n\n## 4. Labels and splits\n\n- **Training labels:** rule-based weak labels from `configs/weak_labels.json`:\n  - built-up gain or loss with a registration tolerance;\n  - vegetation and water changes as hard negatives;\n  - haze, previously vegetated land and tiny blobs as ambiguous.\n- **Evaluation reference:** a blind human review of all 306 candidate\n  windows (`data/labels/reviews/review_labels.json`).\n  - 24 contain man-made change (16 construction, 7 road, 1 demolition).\n  - 62 are uncertain.\n  - The rest are vegetation (126), acquisition artefacts (74), water (14)\n    and no change (6).\n- **Splits:** whole 10 km blocks, 50/25/25 per city, a 2 km training\n  buffer, and one untouched zero-shot block per city (Navi Mumbai/Panvel;\n  Noida). With only 2 validation positives, the window threshold is also\n  checked by 5-fold block-grouped cross-validation.\n\n## 5. **Assistant (claude-opus-5-5) · 2026-11-18T11:36:23.671Z**

Both are done and pushed. Percentile calibration recovered most of the small-change loss without slowing the INT8 models.

| | MinMax (before) | Percentile (now) |
|---|---:|---:|
| Ground model: small changes still detected after INT8 | 85.0% | **89.6%** |
| Ground model: FP32 vs INT8 change overlap (IoU) | 0.83 | **0.89** |
| Full Mumbai / Delhi tiles: FP32 vs INT8 change overlap | 0.80 / 0.83 | **0.87 / 0.90** |
| Onboard model: small changes still detected after INT8 | 76.2% | **87.4%** |
| Ground model: test F1 | 0.200 | 0.192 |

- **No speed cost:** both versions ran at the same speed when compared in the same session (125 vs 128 ms per window).
- **Test F1:** the drop for the ground model comes from two more farmland windows being flagged. Real-change hits are unchanged (5 of 6), and the difference is well within noise.
- **No test leakage:** I picked the variant only by how closely INT8 matches FP32, which needs no human labels. That reasoning is recorded as decision D-017.
- **Register:** the detection register still uses FP32, because INT8 still misses about 10% of the smallest changes.

**Latency is inflated in this run.** QGIS and VS Code were using the CPU, so this run measured 151 ms per window for the ground FP32 model against 124 ms earlier, and INT8 slowed similarly. The docs report FP32 vs INT8 within the same run (INT8 is 1.25–1.5× faster) and explain the background load. For cleaner absolute numbers, close QGIS and re-run `src.quantization.quantize --runs outputs/models/ground_ce_final outputs/models/onboard_final`, which takes about 7 minutes.

**Other updates:**
- I re-quantized both models and regenerated the INT8 full-tile maps and context grids.
- The MinMax results are archived in `outputs/experiments/quantization/`.
- The technical report, Phase 5 doc, decision log and `PROJECT_SUMMARY.md` all reflect the new numbers.
- All 52 tests pass.

I committed these directly on `main`, since you'd already merged and asked for a push.

**Remaining:**
- This reply isn't in the AI usage record yet. As your very last step, re-run `src.utils.export_ai_usage --redact <your first name>`, then commit and push.
- Leave-one-city-out training is still optional and would take about 35 minutes.

It is a confounded stress test: city, season\n(Dec->May vs Oct->Oct), interval (140 vs 1,112 days), haze and land cover all\nchange together.\n\nProtocol (no target-city information is used for training or tuning):\n- ground_ce_loco_train_<source>: same recipe as ground_ce_final, trained and\n  normalised on the source city's patches only (configs/training/).\n- The window threshold k and the window context filter (fit + threshold)\n  are chosen on all decided reviewed windows of the source city.\n- Metrics are computed on all decided reviewed windows of the target city.\n- Reference on the same city: the in-city model ground_ce_final and the rule\n  baseline, compared on the target city's held-out (val/test/zero-shot)\n  windows, where ground_ce_final never trained.\n\nWrites outputs/evaluation/loco.json and loco.md.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport numpy as np\n\nfrom src.evaluation.evaluate import (NATURAL, np.ndarray:\n    predictor, _ = torch_predictor(MODELS / run)\n    return change_map(predict_windows(predictor, x), valid)\n\n\ndef _score(change, truth, cls, sel, k) -> dict:\n    natural = sel & 364:## 6. Change analysis and validation
"""Reproducible grid search (fixed seed, fixed patch bank) for both models.

Stage 1 (ground): loss in {ce, wce, wce_dice} x lr in {1e-3, 3e-3}.
Stage 2 (ground): best stage-1 config without radiometric jitter (ablation).
Stage 3 (onboard): wce_dice at lr in {1e-3, 3e-3}.
Ranking uses weak-validation change F1 only; final selection between the
top configurations is confirmed on human-reviewed validation windows.
Results: outputs/experiments/hparam_search.csv
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import csv
import json
from pathlib import Path

from src.training.train import train

ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
OUT = ROOT / "outputs" / "experiments"
EPOCHS = 6
FIELDS = ["name", "model", "loss", "lr", "radiometric_jitter", "epochs", "parameters",
          "best_epoch", "weakval_precision", "weakval_recall", "weakval_f1",
          "weakval_iou", "train_seconds"]


def _row(result: dict) -> dict:
    cfg, best = result["config"], result["best_weakval"]
    return {"name": cfg["name"], "model": cfg["model"], "loss": cfg["loss"], "lr": cfg["lr"],
            "radiometric_jitter": cfg["radiometric_jitter"], "epochs": cfg["epochs"],
            "parameters": result["parameters"], "best_epoch": best["epoch"],
            **{f"weakval_{k}": round(best[k], 4) for k in ("precision", "recall", "f1", "iou")},
            "train_seconds": result["train_seconds"]}


def run(configs: list[dict], rows: list[dict], path: Path) -> None:
    done = {r["name"] for r in rows}
    for cfg in configs:
        if cfg["name"] in done:
            continue
        rows.append(_row(train({**cfg, "epochs": EPOCHS})))
        with path.open("w", newline="", encoding="utf-8") as handle:
            writer = csv.DictWriter(handle, fieldnames=FIELDS)

 "content": "### Leave-one-city-out (D-018)\n\nTemporal folds are impossible with one pair per city. Leave-one-city-out\n(LOCO) is the closest substitute: train in one city, test in the other. It\nis a confounded stress test, because city, season (Dec → May vs Oct → Oct),\ninterval (140 vs 1,112 days), haze and land cover all change together.\n\nProtocol (`src/evaluation/loco.py`, `configs/training/ground_ce_loco_train_*.json`):\n\n- Same recipe as `ground_ce_final`, trained and normalised on one city's\n  patches only (Mumbai 953 / Delhi 970 patches, about 6.5 min each).\n- k and the window context filter (fit and threshold) are chosen on **all**\n  decided reviewed windows of the source city. Nothing from the target city\n  is used.\n- Metrics are computed on all decided reviewed windows of the target city.\n- The in-city model is compared on the target city's held-out windows,\n  where it never trained.\n\nThe source cities' input distributions differ (mean NIR reflectance 0.169\nin Mumbai vs 0.256 in Delhi). Mumbai has only 4 positive windows (1 held-out)\nand Delhi has 20 (9 held-out).\n\n| Direction | Target windows | Method | P | R | F1 (95 % CI) | Natural false-alarm rate | AUROC (95 % CI) |\n|---|---|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---|\n| Mumbai → Delhi | all Delhi (167, 20 pos.) | LOCO pixel model (k = 8) | 0.121 | 0.95 | 0.215 [0.139, 0.290] | 0.944 | 0.301 [0.208, 0.402] |\n| | | Rule baseline (k = 128) | 0.097 | 0.75 | 0.172 [0.098, 0.244] | 0.944 | 0.206 [0.114, 0.319] |\n| | | LOCO window filter | 0.152 | 0.75 | 0.252 [0.147, 0.353] | 0.573 | 0.602 [0.429, 0.759] |\n| | Delhi held-out (83, 9 pos.) | LOCO pixel model | 0.118 | 1.00 | 0.212 [0.100, 0.323] | 0.917 | 0.406 [0.247, 0.574] |\n| | | In-city `ground_ce_final` | 0.108 | 0.78 | 0.189 [0.063, 0.317] | 0.792 | 0.268 [0.149, 0.392] |\n| | | Rule baseline | 0.090 | 0.78 | 0.161 [0.051, 0.274] | 0.958 | 0.324 [0.156, 0.508] |\n| Delhi → Mumbai | all Mumbai (77, 4 pos.) | LOCO pixel model (k = 1) | 0.087 | 1.00 | 0.160 [0.039, 0.305] | 0.563 | 0.527 [0.383, 0.680] |\n| | | Rule baseline (k = 16) | 0.087 | 1.00 | 0.160 [0.039, 0.305] | 0.563 | 0.545 [0.400, 0.697] |\n| | | LOCO window filter | 0.054 | 0.50 | 0.098 [0, 0.229] | 0.479 | 0.459 [0.312, 0.615] |\n\nFull table, including the Mumbai held-out subset (1 positive):\n`outputs/evaluation/loco.md` and `loco.json`.\n\nWhat LOCO shows:\n\n- **Pixel models carry no transferable skill, because they had none in\n  either city.** The cross-city F1 equals flagging every window (Delhi\n  prevalence 12 % gives F1 0.21). **Assistant (claude-opus-5-5) · 2026-11-18T12:04:41.214Z**

Leave-one-city-out is done and pushed: training took 13 minutes and the evaluation 1.5 minutes. It confirms the main finding and adds one new limitation.

| Mumbai → Delhi (167 windows, 20 real changes) | F1 (95% CI) | AUROC (95% CI) |
|---|---|---|
| Mumbai-trained pixel model | 0.215 [0.139, 0.290] | 0.30 [0.21, 0.40] |
| Rule baseline | 0.172 [0.098, 0.244] | 0.21 [0.11, 0.32] |
| Window filter fitted on Mumbai | 0.252 [0.147, 0.353] | 0.60 [0.43, 0.76] |

- **The pixel model can't transfer what it never had.** Its cross-city F1 equals flagging every window.
- **Training in the target city doesn't help.** On the same Delhi held-out windows, the Delhi-trained model is no better than the Mumbai-trained one (AUROC 0.27 vs 0.41), and all the intervals overlap.
- **New limitation: the window filter is city-specific.** It is the one part that worked in-city (AUROC 0.76), but it transfers only weakly from Mumbai to Delhi (0.60, interval includes 0.5) and not at all from Delhi to Mumbai (0.46). A new city would need its own reviewed windows. That is recorded as decision D-018 and added to the limitations.
- **Delhi → Mumbai is nearly uninformative:** Mumbai has only 4 real-change windows, 1 of them held out.

**Hyperparameter tuning in the report:** §5 is now "Labels, training, and hyperparameter tuning". It has subsections for the training configuration, the search (protocol, search space, the full 9-run table, compute time and findings), the final choice against baselines, and natural false positives.

Everything is pushed to `main`: code, configs, results, the docs (report, Phase 5, decision log, README, project summary) and the refreshed AI record. All 53 tests pass.

# Folder Explanation: Interview Task - Man-Made Change Detection

This folder contains a Sentinel-2 L1C dataset package and the assignment document for a geospatial change-detection task. The goal is to build a reproducible system that detects structural man-made changes between satellite observations while separating them from natural variation, seasonal effects, and acquisition differences.

## 1. Folder overview

At the root of the folder are:

- `Interview Assignment _ Man Made Change Detection.docx`  
  The project brief / assignment specification.

- Four Sentinel-2 `.SAFE` products:
  - `S2A_MSIL1C_20221223T054241_N0510_R005_T43QBB_20240809T025636.SAFE`
  - `S2A_MSIL1C_20230512T053641_N0510_R005_T43QBB_20240908T023323.SAFE`
  - `S2A_MSIL1C_20231006T052701_N0510_R105_T43RGM_20241101T065943.SAFE`
  - `S2A_MSIL1C_20261022T053241_N0513_R105_T43RGM_20261022T091024.SAFE`

- Four matching `.zip` archives for each `.SAFE` dataset.

The folder is structured as a set of satellite image products, not as source code. It is a study dataset for multi-temporal man-made land-cover change analysis.

1	"""Export every AI chat used for this repository into Markdown (assignment AI record).
2	
3	Sources:
4	  * GitHub Copilot (VS Code agent host) sessions under ~/.copilot/session-state
5	    whose recorded working directory is this repository.
6	  * Claude Code sessions under ~/.claude/projects/<this repository>.
7	
8	User prompts and assistant replies are written in full and in time order.
9	Tool calls and tool results are included but truncated (marked as such);
10	text attachments (e.g. pasted text) are inlined. Tool-injected system context
11	is removed and e-mail addresses are redacted. Re-run at the end of the work to
12	capture the latest exchanges.
13	"""
14	
15	from __future__ import annotations
16	
17	import argparse
18	import json
19	import re
20	from collections import Counter
21	from pathlib import Path
22	
23	ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]
24	HOME = Path.home()
25	COPILOT = HOME / ".copilot" / "session-state"
26	CLAUDE = HOME / ".claude" / "projects"
27	OUT_DIR = ROOT / "docs" / "ai_usage"
28	TOOL_ARGS_LIMIT = 3000
29	TOOL_RESULT_LIMIT = 2000
30	EMAIL = re.compile(r"[A-Za-z0-9._%+-]+@[A-Za-z0-9.-]+\.[A-Za-z]{2,}")
31	INJECTED = re.compile(r"<(system-reminder|system_reminder)>.*?</\1>", re.S)
32	
33	
34	EXTRA_REDACTIONS: list[re.Pattern] = []
35	
36	
37	def clean(text: str) -> str:
38	    text = INJECTED.sub("", text or "")
39	    text = EMAIL.sub("[email redacted]", text)
40	    for pattern in EXTRA_REDACTIONS:
41	        text = pattern.sub("[redacted]", text)
42	    return text.strip()
43	
44	
45	def clip(text: str, limit: int) -> str:
46	    text = clean(text)
47	    if len(text) <= limit:
48	        return text
49	    return text[:limit] + f"\n... [truncated {len(text) - limit} characters]"
50	
51	
52	def fence(text: str) -> str:
53	    ticks = "````" if "```" in text else "```"
54	    return f"{ticks}\n{text}\n{ticks}"
55	
56	
57	def same_repo(path: str) -> bool:
58	    return Path(path).resolve() == ROOT.resolve()
59	
60	
61	# --------

 "content": "\"\"\"Independent check of residual registration error on stable features.\n\nThe registration (phase2.py) fits an affine transform to ORB tie points found\non a downsampled preview and reports residuals on those same points. This\nmodule measures the residual shift independently, with a different method,\nat full resolution:\n\n- Candidate checkpoints: 64 x 64 px windows on a 128 px grid that are almost\n  entirely valid in both dates, labelled stable by the weak-label rules (no\n  built-up, vegetation or water change, no haze/ambiguity) and textured\n  (B08 standard deviation >= TEXTURE_MIN), i.e. stable features such as\n  existing built-up areas, roads and field boundaries.\n- Shift: sub-pixel phase cross-correlation (scikit-image, upsample 20,\n  Hanning window) of the earlier date against the later date, separately in\n  B04 and B08. A checkpoint is accepted when the two bands agree within\n  BAND_AGREE_PX (rejects windows where real change or noise dominates).\n  Precision of the method on synthetic shifts: RMSE 0.12 px.\n- Compared variants of the later image: unregistered L2A stack, the default\n  registration (outputs/registered/) and the 15 m-tolerance registration\n  (outputs/registered_15m/).\n\nTie-point locations were not saved, so checkpoints cannot be spatially\nexcluded from them; independence comes from the different scale (full\nresolution vs preview), method (area correlation vs feature matching) and\nsample (thousands of windows vs ~300 points).\n\nOutputs: outputs/reports/registration_checkpoints.csv (per window and\nvariant) and outputs/reports/registration_check.{json,md}.\n\"\"\"\n\nfrom __future__ import annotations\n\nimport csv\nimport json\nfrom pathlib import Path\n\nimport cv2\nimport numpy as np\nimport rasterio\nfrom rasterio.windows import Window\nfrom skimage.registration import phase_cross_correlation\n\nfrom src.training.generate_candidates import NODATA, SCENES, _find_one, _pair_paths\n\nROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[2]\nOUT = ROOT / \"outputs\" / \"reports\"\nSIZE, STRIDE = 64, 128\nBANDS = {\"B04\": 3, \"B08\": 4}          # 1-based indices in the six-band stack\nVALID_MIN, STABLE_MIN = 0.98, 0.90\nTEXTURE_MIN = 0.02                     # B08 reflectance standard deviation\nBAND_AGREE_PX = 0.5\nMAX_SHIFT_PX = 5.0\nPIXEL_M = 10.0\nGRID = 4                               # coverage cells per side\nHANN = cv2.createHanningWindow((SIZE, SIZE), cv2.CV_32F)\n\n\ndef measure_shift(before: np.ndarray, after: np.ndarray) -> tuple[float, float, float]:\n    \"\"\"Displacement (dx, dy) in px of `after` content relative to `before`, and error.\"\"\"\n    a = (before - before.mean()) * HANN\n    b = (after - after.mean()) * HANN\n    shift, error, _ = phase_cross_correlation(a, b, upsample_factor=20)\n    return float(-shift[1]), float(-shift[0]), float(error)\n\n\ndef variants(scene:

 "new_string": "WARP_PAD = 8\n\n\ndef warp_block(\n    dataset: rasterio.io.DatasetReader,\n    index: int,\n    moving_transform: Affine,\n    window: Window,\n) -> np.ndarray:\n    \"\"\"Resample one band of `dataset` placed at `moving_transform` onto `window`.\n\n    The source is read as a padded ndarray block. Passing ``rasterio.band()``\n    instead makes rasterio take the dataset's own transform and silently\n    ignore ``src_transform``, which turned the first registration run into a\n    copy of the unregistered image (DECISION_LOG D-019).\n    \"\"\"\n    # Source pixel position of the window's corners under the fitted transform.\n    inverse = ~(~dataset.transform * moving_transform)\n    corners = [inverse * (window.col_off + dx, window.row_off + dy)\n               for dx in (0, window.width) for dy in (0, window.height)]\n    shift = max(max(abs(cx - (window.col_off + dx)), abs(cy - (window.row_off + dy)))\n                for (cx, cy), (dx, dy) in zip(corners, [(dx, dy) for dx in (0, window.width)\n                                                       for dy in (0, window.height)]))\n    if shift > WARP_PAD - 1:\n        raise ValueError(f\"registration shift {shift:.1f} px exceeds the warp padding\")\n    source_window = Window(window.col_off - WARP_PAD, window.row_off - WARP_PAD,\n                           window.width + 2 * WARP_PAD, window.height + 2 * WARP_PAD)\n    nodata = NODATA if dataset.nodata is None else dataset.nodata\n    source = dataset.read(index, window=source_window, boundless=True,\n                          fill_value=nodata).astype(np.float32)\n    block = np.full((int(window.height), int(window.width)), NODATA, dtype=np.float32)\n    reproject(\n        source=source,\n        destination=block,\n        src_transform=moving_transform * Affine.translation(source_window.col_off, source_window.row_off),\n        src_crs=dataset.crs,\n        src_nodata=nodata,\n        dst_transform=dataset.window_transform(window),\n        dst_crs=dataset.crs,\n        dst_nodata=NODATA,\n        resampling=Resampling.bilinear,\n        init_dest_nodata=True,\n    )\n    return block\n\n\ndef register_pair("
